20 Nov 2026Not stated in the abstract reviewed

Beyond Correlation: A Causality-Driven Machine Learning Framework to Climate-Crop Yield Data Using Transfer Entropy and Lag Optimization Approaches

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Transfer entropy screened climate variables and lags, and regressors predicted maize and soybean yields from 40 years of USDA-NASS data for 88 Ohio counties. The held-out results describe observational climate-yield associations; a random split does not establish causality or broad geographic transfer.

Technology: Transfer entropy feature screening and lag selection; machine-learning yield regressors

Editorial relevance 53 climate Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Transfer entropy feature screening and lag selection; machine-learning yield regressors
Test and result
On 3,360 Ohio county-years, held-out random-split prediction reported R² of about 0.75 for maize and 0.72 for soybean, with selected climate variables and lags.
Limitation / still unknown
The analysis is observational and county-year dependent; no temporal or geographic external validation is reported.

15 Nov 2026Not stated in the abstract reviewed

Machine-learning-assisted prediction and optimization of tensile strength in particulate-filled polymer composites

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: LASSO, K-nearest neighbors, support-vector regression and genetic-algorithm-optimized SVR were compared for tensile-strength prediction in metal-powder/resin composites. Evaluation used nested five-fold cross-validation on 45 experimental conditions, so performance is bounded to this dataset.

Technology: LASSO, KNN, SVR and GA-SVR regressors

Editorial relevance 53 materials Published article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
LASSO, KNN, SVR and GA-SVR regressors
Test and result
A 45-condition metal-powder/resin composite dataset was used to predict tensile strength; nested five-fold cross-validation compared the models.
Limitation / still unknown
Small dataset; cross-validation is internal and does not establish performance on independently produced composites.

7 Nov 2026Not stated in the abstract reviewed

Unified Factored Representations Resist Catastrophic Forgetting

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Across a one-dimensional regression task and an image-regression task, networks with unified factored representations lost less performance than fractured-entangled counterparts during repeated fine-tuning on compatible variations. The evidence is limited to the tested tasks and setups.

Technology: Unified factored neural representations; representation-distillation training loss

Editorial relevance 53 computer science PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Unified factored neural representations; representation-distillation training loss
Test and result
Controlled comparisons in two domains found substantially less forgetting for unified factored representations; distillation into fresh networks also reduced forgetting.
Limitation / still unknown
The experiments cover selected regression and image tasks; broader architectures and continual-learning settings remain unestablished.

5 Nov 2026Not stated in the abstract reviewed

DUOS meta-learns update rules for opponent shaping in simulated games

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: DUOS meta-trains an update rule that learns from an agent’s own experience without access to opponent parameters, policies, rewards or learning rules at deployment. In simulated general-sum games it ranked first in normalized mean score and self-play among tournament entrants; this is limited to the reported games.

Technology: Discovered Update Rule for Opponent Shaping (DUOS), a meta-learned update rule

Editorial relevance 53 computer science PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Discovered Update Rule for Opponent Shaping (DUOS), a meta-learned update rule
Test and result
The arXiv abstract reports first-place normalized mean score and self-play among tournament entrants; the evaluation is in simulated games.
Limitation / still unknown
The tournament and game simulations do not establish behavior with human opponents or real-world strategic settings.

5 Nov 2026Not stated in the abstract reviewed

Beyond Instruction Override: Benchmarking Indirect Prompt Injection with Task-Tampering Attacks

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A benchmark of 90 validated cases across three tool-use environments evaluated whether existing defenses covered attacks that tamper with decisions within a user’s task. Detection-and-removal reduced objective-targeting success more than observation- and state-targeting success. This is a defensive benchmark result; attack construction details are omitted.

Technology: Tool-using language models and detection/removal and tool-policy defenses

Editorial relevance 53 computer science PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Tool-using language models and detection/removal and tool-policy defenses
Test and result
In 90 benchmark cases across three tool-use environments, detection-and-removal reduced objective-targeting success to 2.4%, while observation-targeting and state-targeting success remained 9.5% and 12.1%.
Limitation / still unknown
Results are benchmark-specific and do not establish operational deployment or general defense rates. Attack construction details are intentionally omitted.

4 Nov 2026Not stated in the abstract reviewed

Data-Driven Modeling of Molten Chloride Corrosion Using Static Test Data

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Models trained on 291 literature-derived static corrosion tests identified salt-preparation information as an important predictor. Encoding preparation with a graded index improved reported predictions, but the analysis is retrospective and does not establish causal mechanisms or operational performance.

Technology: Seven machine-learning regressors; chloride salt preparation index

Editorial relevance 53 materials PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Seven machine-learning regressors; chloride salt preparation index
Test and result
Repeated five-fold cross-validation and out-of-fold conformal calibration assessed corrosion-rate prediction; the graded preparation descriptor improved model performance.
Limitation / still unknown
Data are literature-derived static tests with missing and inconsistent measurements; no operational-system evaluation is reported.

Automated, Radiation-Free Detection of Cochlear Implant Electrode Array Migration Using Routine Impedance Telemetry.

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A random-forest model used routine impedance telemetry to detect cochlear implant electrode-array migration against radiological reference findings in a retrospective clinical study. The result is a study-specific detection evaluation; it does not establish that radiation can be safely omitted in clinical care.

Limitation / still unknown

HLO-AMP: Hierarchical Loco-Manipulation from Object-Aware Motion Priors

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A hierarchical humanoid controller used an object-aware motion-imitation policy and was evaluated on real box-handling tasks across payloads and perturbations. Hardware trials support performance in the tested tasks, while failures at higher payloads bound the result.

Technology: HLO-AMP hierarchical locomotion-and-manipulation policy with object-aware motion priors

Editorial relevance 60 computer science PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

AI contribution
HLO-AMP hierarchical locomotion-and-manipulation policy with object-aware motion priors
Test and result
A deployed policy completed tested hardware box-handling tasks across payloads and perturbations; failures at 7–8 kg were reported.
Limitation / still unknown
Hardware evidence is limited to the tested robot and tasks; performance degraded at the reported higher payloads.

3 Nov 2026Not stated in the abstract reviewed

When Ground Truth Is Not Ground Truth: Automatic Labeling for Hallucination Detection

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Human validation compared automatic supervision labels with human assessments across hallucination-detection methods. Misclassification differed substantially by judge: QGen-9B had the lowest reported rate, while BLEURT-based labels performed worse. This is an evaluation finding, not a universal ranking across tasks.

Technology: Automatic supervision judges including BLEURT and QGen-9B

Editorial relevance 53 computer science PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Automatic supervision judges including BLEURT and QGen-9B
Test and result
Across two QA datasets, two generators and five languages, human validation reported misclassification rates of 12.86% for QGen-9B and about 47% for BLEURT and BLEURT-n.
Limitation / still unknown
The comparison covers the study’s datasets, languages, judges and human-label protocol; rates may not transfer to other settings.

2 Nov 2026Not stated in the abstract reviewed

Evolved Spiking Neural Networks for EEG Seizure Detection: Calcium Dynamics and Delay Mechanics

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: The EONS neuroevolution framework searched spiking-network topology and parameters for EEG seizure detection. Per-neuron calcium improved held-out CHB-MIT results and showed a smaller result in patient-disjoint Siena splits; this supports a bounded detector-search finding, not biological calcium dynamics.

Technology: EONS neuroevolution of spiking neural networks with integrate-and-fire calcium currents

Editorial relevance 53 medicine PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
EONS neuroevolution of spiking neural networks with integrate-and-fire calcium currents
Test and result
On 10 held-out CHB-MIT patients, per-neuron calcium raised MCC from 0.592 to 0.726 across 20 runs; Siena patient-disjoint splits showed gains of +0.096 and +0.059 MCC.
Limitation / still unknown
More datasets and neuromorphic-hardware testing are needed; results do not establish clinical deployment.

1 Nov 2026Not stated in the abstract reviewed

When the Text Is Silent: Memory Shortcuts and the Validity of LLM Annotation under Evidence Insufficiency

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: In controlled counterfactual texts about public figures and matched control names, three commercial LLMs produced labels unsupported by the text when evidence was absent. These memory shortcuts were often factually correct about a namesake but methodologically invalid as text-grounded annotations.

Technology: Three commercial large language models

Editorial relevance 53 computer science PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Three commercial large language models
Test and result
Across 995 public figures and four matched control names per figure, three commercial LLMs were evaluated on 121,594 prompts each. When text evidence was absent, one model increased remembered occupations, ages and countries by 17.0, 14.9 and 6.8 percentage points relative to synthetic names.
Limitation / still unknown
The results use selected models, entities and constructed prompts; they do not quantify all annotation settings.

1 Nov 2026Not stated in the abstract reviewed

From Single-Agent Self-Discovery to Multi-Agent Adaptation: Scaling Laws of Partner Feedback and Convention Rigidity in Meta Reinforcement Learning Algorithms

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Across more than 1,800 runs in simulated collective-robotics and coordination tasks, partner swaps revealed that apparent team-return gains could conceal lower focal-agent performance. Results also showed sensitivity to reciprocal partner feedback; they are simulation findings.

Technology: Disco103 and OPEN meta-learned reinforcement-learning update rules

Editorial relevance 53 computer science PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Disco103 and OPEN meta-learned reinforcement-learning update rules
Test and result
Across 1,800+ simulated runs, four-cell partner swaps found an apparent +1.066 team-return gain alongside a −0.119 focal-agent loss in a reported setting.
Limitation / still unknown
The results concern specified VMAS and Overcooked simulations; they do not establish performance in physical multi-agent systems.

30 Oct 2026Not stated in the abstract reviewed

Learning to evolve by matching velocities

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: The study compares simulation-free velocity matching with trajectory-based learning objectives on selected ODE and PDE systems. It reports that discretization and trajectory fitting can create spurious learned equilibria; the target equations are known, so this is a methods result rather than discovery of new natural dynamics.

Technology: Neural networks trained by velocity matching; trajectory-fitting objectives

Editorial relevance 53 computer science PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Neural networks trained by velocity matching; trajectory-fitting objectives
Test and result
Mathematical analysis and simulations on selected known ODE/PDE systems compare the learned velocity fields and equilibrium behavior.
Limitation / still unknown
Evidence concerns the equations and discretization settings studied; it does not establish new physical dynamics.

30 Oct 2026Not stated in the abstract reviewed

Generative Design and Graph Neural Network Screening of Novel Bimetallic Alloy Catalysts for the Hydrogen Evolution Reaction

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: MatterGen-generated structures and learned screening models nominated bimetallic alloy candidates; density-functional theory calculations evaluated a shortlist for hydrogen binding. Four candidates had near-thermoneutral predicted binding, but catalytic activity has not been experimentally tested.

Technology: MatterGen; MatterSim; MEGNet; ALIGNN

Family MatterGen: profile · models page

Editorial relevance 53 chemistry PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
MatterGen generated alloy structures; MatterSim screened/relaxed surfaces; MEGNet and ALIGNN screened adsorption candidates before DFT evaluation.
Test and result
MatterGen, MatterSim, MEGNet and ALIGNN screened candidates; plane-wave DFT found four alloys with near-thermoneutral hydrogen binding, and phonon calculations found the eight evaluated alloys dynamically stable.
Limitation / still unknown
No experimental catalytic activity or durability has been demonstrated; the candidates are proposed for experimental follow-up.

30 Oct 2026Not stated in the abstract reviewed

Attention Maps Know When to Think: Training-Free Difficulty-Aware Chain-of-Thought for Reasoning Segmentation

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Attention-map entropy was used as a training-free difficulty signal to choose whether a multimodal language model should use chain-of-thought reasoning for reasoning segmentation. The framework also used bidirectional rethinking when initial output failed; benchmark results and efficiency are limited to the reported tasks.

Technology: Attention-map entropy used as a difficulty signal for reasoning segmentation

Editorial relevance 53 computer science PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Attention-map entropy from a multimodal language model signals task difficulty and determines whether to trigger chain-of-thought; bidirectional rethinking can reverse the decision after a failed output.
Test and result
DA-CoT used attention entropy to gate chain-of-thought and reported state-of-the-art performance on the reported reasoning-segmentation benchmarks with improved inference efficiency.
Limitation / still unknown
The benchmark result is limited to the evaluated models and tasks; it does not establish general reasoning gains across modalities or applications.

29 Oct 2026Not stated in the abstract reviewed

Elucidating the Design Space of LM Data Attribution

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A controlled attribution benchmark fine-tuned language models on fictitious knowledge, allowing ground-truth source tracing while varying redundancy and lexical distractors. Standard document-level averaging diluted localized attribution evidence; negative-log-odds gradients with top-token aggregation improved recall in the evaluated benchmarks.

Technology: Language models with token-level data-attribution scoring

Editorial relevance 53 computer science PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Token-level language-model attribution scores were evaluated under controlled fictitious-knowledge, redundancy and distractor settings.
Test and result
On CATT, top-token aggregation with negative-log-odds gradients improved ground-truth recall by 35–72 percentage points across several baselines; the correction also improved counterfactual retraining performance on FTRACE-TREX by up to 4.9-fold.
Limitation / still unknown
These results use controlled attribution benchmarks and do not establish reliable provenance for arbitrary language-model training data.
Evidence sources and annotation context

29 Oct 2026Not stated in the abstract reviewed

The Verification Wall in Microbial Metabolomics

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A computational audit assessed confirmation routes for 10,645 database entries of computationally inferred microbial metabolites. Depending on thresholds, 5–37% had no route identified; this spread reflects analytical assumptions and does not constitute a measured count of unsynthesizable compounds or experimental confirmation.

6 Oct 2026Alexandr Grebennikov (affiliation not stated in arXiv record)

GPT-6 Pro proves an optimal anticoncentration bound for polynomial Littlewood–Offord inequalities

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: The source presents a proof discovered autonomously by GPT-6 Pro establishing an O_d(r^{-1/2}) zero-probability bound for degree-d multilinear polynomials with r disjoint degree-d monomials, improving the prior bound by removing a polylogarithmic factor and resolving the Nguyen–Vu conjecture. The key argument also proves a total-influence estimate for bounded-degree rational functions, resolving a recent conjecture of Kothari, Kovacs-Deak, Wang and Yang; these are one connected paper-level result, not separate entries.

17 Sep 2026Xinglong Wu (author affiliation not stated in the arXiv record)

GPT-5.6 constructs a counterexample to Talagrand’s operator cotype problem

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: The paper gives a negative answer, already for q=2, to Talagrand’s question whether an operator’s Rademacher cotype is universally bounded by its Gaussian cotype and its (q,1)-summing norm. For every sufficiently large power-of-two dimension it constructs a Banach space and operator violating such a universal bound. The same construction also yields a negative answer to Kwapień’s decomposition problem.

Technology: ChatGPT GPT-5.6

Family GPT-5: profile · models page

Editorial relevance 60 mathematics PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The author states that the counterexample was discovered during discussions with GPT-5.6. The paper presents an explicit Walsh–Hadamard-based construction and proof; the author independently verified the arguments.
Test and result
The paper gives a negative answer, already for q=2, to Talagrand’s question whether an operator’s Rademacher cotype is universally bounded by its Gaussian cotype and its (q,1)-summing norm.
Limitation / still unknown
This is a single-author arXiv preprint, not a peer-reviewed publication. GPT-5.6 is credited with discovering the counterexample during discussions; the author says they independently verified all arguments. No Lean or other proof-assistant formalization is reported. The mathematical scope is the stated q=2 counterexample and its stated corollary, not a complete characterization of operator cotype.

28 Apr 2025Not stated in the abstract reviewed

A Simple, Interpretable Machine Learning Model Based on Clinical Factors Accurately Predicts Incident Dysplasia or Malignancy in Barrett's Esophagus.

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A decision tree trained on a retrospective Barrett esophagus cohort identified segment length alone as a useful risk-stratification predictor for observed progression. In 598 patients (61 progressors), the held-out test set achieved AUROC .79; lowering the threshold raised sensitivity to 93.3% with specificity 58.5%. This is single-center retrospective evidence and is not an externally validated surveillance recommendation.

Technology: Supervised decision tree among six ML classifiers uses five candidate predictors; the selected tree relies only on Barrett segment length.

Editorial relevance 53 medicine Published article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Supervised decision tree among six ML classifiers uses five candidate predictors; the selected tree relies only on Barrett segment length.
Test and result
In 598 patients with follow-up through Jan 2024, 61 progressed within five years. The tree’s held-out test AUROC was .79; default sensitivity/specificity were 73.3%/70.4%, and a lower threshold yielded 93.3% sensitivity/58.5% specificity; segment-length thresholds define four risk groups.
Limitation / still unknown
The model was developed on 75% of a single-center veteran cohort and tested on a 25% held-out split (n=150); progressor pathology was independently verified by a second expert pathologist. No external cohort was used, calibration was suboptimal, and the sensitivity threshold trades specificity for fewer false negatives.

28 Feb 2023Not stated in the abstract reviewed

Artificial intelligence (AI) for breast cancer screening: BreastScreen population-based cohort study of cancer detection.

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A commercially available mammography AI algorithm scored cancer likelihood and was compared with practicing radiologists; simulated AI-radiologist arbitration estimated workload and screening outcomes. External evaluation of 108,970 consecutive screens with registry-ascertained interval cancers: AI AUC .83 vs radiologists .93; at prospective threshold sensitivity .67 vs .68 but specificity .81 vs .97. AI-plus-reader simulation reduced reading volume 41.4%, with slightly lower cancer detection rate but detected interval cancers missed at original reading. Real-world retrospective population screening cohort external validation, outcomes linked to registry; simulations are not prospective deployment.

Technology: A commercially available mammography AI algorithm scored cancer likelihood and was compared with practicing radiologists; simulated AI-radiologist arbitration estimated workload and screening outcomes.

27 May 2022Not stated in the abstract reviewed

Predicting Van der Waals Heterostructures by a Combined Machine Learning and Density Functional Theory Approach.

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: The full primary article combines DFT calculations on 595 electronic-property bilayers and 776 distance/binding-energy bilayers with supervised ML trained from constituent monolayer descriptors. The resulting survey of nearly 4,000 bilayers reports property predictions and a specific stacking-dependent result: most AA/AB pairs differ substantially in equilibrium spacing, implying reconstruction in twisted moiré systems. Repeated five-fold validation supports screening utility; findings are computational predictions, not experimental confirmation.

Technology: Supervised ML regressors trained on DFT-labeled vdW bilayers and monolayer descriptors predict band gap, electron affinity, ionization energy, interlayer distance and binding energy across unlabeled combinations.

Editorial relevance 60 multidisciplinary Published article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Supervised ML regressors trained on DFT-labeled vdW bilayers and monolayer descriptors predict band gap, electron affinity, ionization energy, interlayer distance and binding energy across unlabeled combinations.
Test and result
Survey of nearly 4,000 bilayers from seven prototypes; reports low-error predictions and the specific AA/AB stacking difference in spacing, with implications for moiré reconstruction.
Limitation / still unknown
Models are evaluated with repeated k-fold CV and compared against DFT; training labels originate from DFT calculations. Scope remains computational; no experimental validation is claimed.

29 Sep 2021Not stated in the abstract reviewed

Prediction of storage time in different seafood based on color values with artificial neural network modeling.

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A one-hidden-layer, 30-neuron feedforward ANN uses seafood colour coordinates (L*, a*, b*) to predict storage time. The primary paper compiled 205 literature datapoints and evaluated the model on an additional 45 literature datapoints from other seafood studies; the article reports R²=0.81 for the selected topology and a predicted/observed regression coefficient of 0.85 (the abstract rounds R² to 0.80). This is a narrow food-quality prediction result, not new measurements, and the pooled literature-derived validation is not prospective field validation.

Technology: A feedforward MLP uses measured L*, a*, b* values as inputs and one hidden layer to output storage time; topology was chosen by comparing R² and MSE.

Editorial relevance 60 computer science Published article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
A feedforward MLP uses measured L*, a*, b* values as inputs and one hidden layer to output storage time; topology was chosen by comparing R² and MSE.
Test and result
The selected 30-neuron topology reports R²=0.81 and MSE=0.20 during optimization; the paper separately reports a predicted/observed regression coefficient of 0.85 (abstract R² rounded to 0.80).
Limitation / still unknown
205 literature experiments were split into 145/30/30 train/test/validation; a distinct additional 45 literature datapoints from other species/studies were also used as validation. The evidence is pooled retrospective data, not prospective measured validation, and species/process heterogeneity limits generalization.

30 Apr 2021Not stated in the abstract reviewed

Computationally Derived Cribriform Area Index from Prostate Cancer Hematoxylin and Eosin Images Is Associated with Biochemical Recurrence Following Radical Prostatectomy and Is Most Prognostic in Gleason Grade Group 2.

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A UNet-inspired deep-learning model segmented invasive cribriform carcinoma on prostate H&E slides, enabling a computed cribriform area index (CAI). In a four-site cohort, higher CAI was associated with biochemical recurrence among ICC-positive cases (adjusted HR 1.19 per doubling, p=0.018); discrimination was modest and the association was weak in the overall cohort.

Technology: A UNet-inspired deep-learning segmentation model was trained on 325 annotated image tiles from 36 patients and applied to tumor regions; CAI is the proportion of annotated tumor area classified as cribriform carcinoma.

Editorial relevance 53 medicine Published article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
A UNet-inspired deep-learning segmentation model was trained on 325 annotated image tiles from 36 patients and applied to tumor regions; CAI is the proportion of annotated tumor area classified as cribriform carcinoma.
Test and result
Among 411 patients with ICC morphology, CAI had c-index 0.62; doubling CAI was independently associated with biochemical recurrence among 298 ICC-positive patients (HR 1.19, 95% CI 1.03–1.38, p=0.018). Overall-cohort discrimination was weak (c-index 0.54).
Limitation / still unknown
The segmentation model was applied to a multi-institutional validation cohort of 749 patients; the source reports site-level patterns and recurrence associations. The modest discrimination and retrospective association do not establish clinical utility.

23 Jan 2020Not stated in the abstract reviewed

Spatial Pattern and Environmental Drivers of Acid Phosphatase Activity in Europe.

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding / tested result: Back-propagation neural networks scaled laboratory-measured acid phosphatase activity from 126 European sites using environmental drivers; leave-one-out evaluation explained 58% of variability (RMSE 6.83 after excluding nine outliers) and identified climate/nutrient gradients. This is a model-based spatial ecological result, not causal attribution.

Technology: BPN and random forests infer spatial patterns from site measurements and environmental predictors.

Editorial relevance 60 climate Published article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
BPN and random forests infer spatial patterns from site measurements and environmental predictors.
Limitation / still unknown
Leave-one-site-out evaluation across 126 sites; outlier exclusion, uncertainty intervals and partial-correlation analysis are reported.
